class="not-prose text-sm text-foreground/50">Last updated:<!-- --> <time dateTime="2026-08-04">August 4, 2026</time></p><h2>What is a GitHub Copilot SaaS boilerplate?</h2>
<p>A <strong>GitHub Copilot SaaS boilerplate</strong> is a production-ready codebase that gives Copilot working authentication, subscriptions, organizations, email, and application structure before it writes your product features. supastarter adds typed architecture, an <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code>, linting, and end-to-end tests, so Copilot can extend known patterns and verify its work.</p>
<p>This is different from asking Agent Mode to generate an entire SaaS from an empty folder. Copilot still writes the feature code, but security-sensitive infrastructure already exists and has documentation it can inspect.</p>
<h2>Why pair GitHub Copilot with supastarter?</h2>
<p>GitHub Copilot can search a repository, edit multiple files, run terminal commands, and iterate after a failed test. Those capabilities are most useful when the repository already tells the agent where code belongs and how completion is measured.</p>
<p>supastarter provides:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Production infrastructure</strong> — authentication, payments, organizations, transactional email, storage, analytics, and an admin dashboard</li>
<li><strong>Repository instructions</strong> — a maintained <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code> describes the monorepo, conventions, commands, and finishing checklist</li>
<li><strong>Typed boundaries</strong> — TypeScript connects the database, API, and frontend so invalid agent changes become visible compiler errors</li>
<li><strong>Examples to copy</strong> — existing schemas, procedures, forms, tables, and tests give Copilot concrete patterns</li>
<li><strong>Runnable verification</strong> — lint, type checks, and Playwright tests let the agent check its own result</li>
</ul>
<p>supastarter is available for Next.js, Nuxt, and TanStack Start as source code with a one-time lifetime license.</p>
<h2>Which GitHub Copilot agent should you use?</h2>
<p>GitHub provides two complementary agent workflows. <a href="https://github.blog/developer-skills/github/less-todo-more-done-the-difference-between-coding-agent-and-agent-mode-in-github-copilot/">Agent Mode</a> works with you inside the editor, while Copilot&#x27;s cloud agent can handle a delegated task in a background environment.</p>
<table><thead><tr><th>Workflow</th><th>Where it runs</th><th>Best SaaS tasks</th><th>Review point</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><strong>Copilot Agent Mode</strong></td><td>Your IDE and local workspace</td><td>Exploring a feature, debugging, UI iteration, migrations</td><td>Continuously while it edits and runs commands</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Copilot cloud agent</strong></td><td>An isolated GitHub-hosted environment</td><td>Scoped issues, tests, routine fixes, parallel backlog work</td><td>The branch or pull request it returns</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Use Agent Mode when requirements are still changing or you need to inspect the application together. Use the cloud agent when the task has clear acceptance criteria and the repository can install, test, and build without manual setup.</p>
<h2>How do you configure Copilot for a SaaS repository?</h2>
<p>Copilot supports repository-wide instructions in <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">.github/copilot-instructions.md</code>, path-specific files under <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">.github/instructions/</code>, and agent instructions in <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code>. GitHub&#x27;s <a href="https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-on-github/customize-copilot/add-custom-instructions/add-repository-instructions">custom-instructions documentation</a> states that the nearest <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code> in the directory tree takes precedence for agent work.</p>
<p>supastarter already includes the cross-tool <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code>. If your team wants a Copilot-specific file too, keep it short and point back to that source of truth:</p>
<pre><code class="language-md"># GitHub Copilot repository instructions

Read and follow AGENTS.md before changing code.
Reuse existing database, API, form, and authorization patterns.
Keep organization-owned data scoped to the active organization.
Do not modify authentication or payment infrastructure unless requested.
Run the repository lint and type-check commands before finishing.
</code></pre>
<p>Path-specific instructions are useful when one package has special constraints. For example, a billing instruction can apply only to payment code, while UI instructions can require existing components and accessibility checks.</p>
<h2>What does a good Copilot SaaS task look like?</h2>
<p>Give Copilot one product capability, point it at an existing pattern, define data ownership, and state the checks it must run. For example:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Read <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code> and inspect the existing organization-scoped API procedures. Add project usage limits: store a monthly project allowance on each plan, prevent project creation after the active organization reaches its limit, return a typed error, and show an upgrade message in the project form. Add tests for allowed and blocked creation. Do not change checkout or webhook handling. Run lint and the relevant tests before finishing.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>That prompt answers the questions agents otherwise guess:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Where is the source of truth?</strong> Existing plan and organization patterns.</li>
<li><strong>What is the security boundary?</strong> The active organization, not the current user alone.</li>
<li><strong>What should change?</strong> Plan configuration, project creation, UI feedback, and tests.</li>
<li><strong>What should not change?</strong> Checkout and webhook infrastructure.</li>
<li><strong>What proves completion?</strong> A typed result plus passing checks.</li>
</ol>
<h2>Which SaaS work should Copilot handle?</h2>
<table><thead><tr><th>Good agent task</th><th>Keep under close human review</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Product CRUD features</td><td>Authentication policy changes</td></tr><tr><td>Dashboard pages and forms</td><td>Tenant-isolation rules</td></tr><tr><td>Tests and fixtures</td><td>Payment webhook behavior</td></tr><tr><td>Refactors with existing patterns</td><td>Destructive database migrations</td></tr><tr><td>Documentation and repetitive cleanup</td><td>Secrets, permissions, and production infrastructure</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Copilot can assist with every item in the right column, but those changes should not be accepted only because the build passes. A valid implementation can still encode the wrong authorization or billing policy.</p>
<h2>Frequently Asked Questions</h2>
<h3>Does GitHub Copilot support AGENTS.md?</h3>
<p>Yes. GitHub Copilot supports <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code> as an agent-instruction format and discovers files according to their location in the repository. supastarter includes a maintained root <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code> in every starter kit.</p>
<h3>Do I need .github/copilot-instructions.md too?</h3>
<p>Not necessarily. Use <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code> as the shared source of truth, then add <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">.github/copilot-instructions.md</code> only for Copilot-specific guidance or features that do not consume agent instructions in the same way.</p>
<h3>Can Copilot&#x27;s cloud agent build a complete SaaS feature?</h3>
<p>Yes, when the feature is scoped, the environment installs automatically, and acceptance criteria are testable. Ambiguous product decisions and changes to auth, billing, or tenant isolation still need active human review.</p>
<h3>Is supastarter only for GitHub Copilot Agent Mode?</h3>
<p>No. The same codebase and <code class="not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre&gt;&amp;]:border-0 [pre&gt;&amp;]:bg-transparent [pre&gt;&amp;]:p-0">AGENTS.md</code> work with <a href="/cursor-boilerplate">Cursor</a>, <a href="/claude-code-boilerplate">Claude Code</a>, <a href="/codex-boilerplate">Codex</a>, Cline, Kiro, and other coding agents.</p>
<h3>Why use a boilerplate instead of asking Copilot to generate everything?</h3>
<p>A boilerplate removes work that is both repetitive and expensive to get wrong. Copilot can focus on your differentiating product features while authentication, subscriptions, organization isolation, and webhook handling start from a tested implementation.</p>

class="block truncate text-xs text-foreground/60">Founder of Spyglasses</small></div></div><div><p class="mt-2 mb-0 line-clamp-2 text-sm text-foreground/60">When we first set out to build Spyglasses we knew we needed an MVP before we even considered fundraising. That led us to researching starter kits. We looked at a dozen before settling on Supastarter. It had all the features we needed plus strong Cursor integration, which was critical to us.
Thanks to Supastarter we saved 7+ months on our development estimates and even managed to pull some nice-to-haves into our initial launch.</p><a href="https://spyglasses.io" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="p-0 text-xs text-primary">View project →</a></div></div></div><div class="min-w-0 flex-[0_0_min(100%,300px)]"><div class="h-full cursor-pointer border-l p-4 pt-5 stroke-width="1.5"></path></svg></div></div></div></div><div><p class="mt-2 mb-0 line-clamp-2 text-sm text-foreground/60">As someone experienced with backend development but with limited frontend experience, I knew from the beginning that I would need to use a starter kit. There were a few options, and I decided to go with supastarter. It turned out to be the right call. Supastarter + AI coding + some common sense and guidance -> the project was shipped much faster than I anticipated. I would definitely use it again and not build things like auth, org and user management, and many other things all SaaS businesses need from scratch. Highly recommended!</p><a href="https://screenshotscout.com" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="p-0 text-xs text-primary">View project →</a></div></div></div><div class="min-w-0 text-foreground/60">Just chose @supastarter as nextjs/supabase boilerplate for my next health/biohacking SaaS over other alternatives. Why ?

- Extensive documentation, while keeping things simple and down to earth
- Clean UI made directly with Radix
- ORM (Prisma)</p></div></div></div><div class="min-w-0 flex-[0_0_min(100%,300px)]"><div class="h-full cursor-pointer border-l p-4 pt-5 shadow-sm transition-shadow hover:shadow-md"><div class="flex The built-in features and integrations saved me hours of development work, and the end result is a polished, professional site that has already attracted over 2,000 UVs within its first week. The support from the Supastarter team has been outstanding, and I truly appreciate their attention to detail and commitment to helping creators succeed. The fact that Supastarter's official Twitter account even gave us a shoutout speaks volumes about their dedication to their users. I highly recommend Supastarter to anyone looking to launch a high-quality website quickly and efficiently.</p><a href="https://domainscore.ai" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="p-0 @phewtab</small></div></div><div><p class="mt-2 mb-0 line-clamp-2 text-sm text-foreground/60">I built the entire backend of my product, Phew AI Tab, using Supastarter, including the user system and payments and other api. Initially, I used Lemonsqueezy but switched to Stripe for specific reasons later on. I can't imagine how much time it would have taken without Supastarter; it only took a few days.

What impressed me the most was the tech support from Supastarter. Even for issues not directly related to Supastarter, Jonathan provided timely and professional responses, which was a huge help and a great learning experience.

Supastarter has grown even more powerful since I first used it. I highly recommend it to everyone starting their business, even up to a medium-sized team, as its comprehensive system can fully support growth.

I'm so glad I chose Supastarter and I'm thankful for the team's support.</p><a href="https://phewtab.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="p-0 text-xs text-primary">View project text-foreground/60">This supastarter has change my life of developer. 

With my CTO we are launching a startup and this boilerplate has really accelerate the development.

As a developer of 10 years experience, I see that this has been built based on strong knowledge of the development covering all aspects, infrastructure, backend frontend with letting always the easiest way to customize. 

I would recommend it at 100% to a friend :) 

Well done Jonathan !</p></div></div></div><div class="min-w-0 flex-[0_0_min(100%,300px)]"><div class="h-full cursor-pointer border-l p-4 pt-5 shadow-sm transition-shadow hover:shadow-md"><div software I was using, which prompted me to report the problem on Discord. To my surprise and delight, the response from the development team was incredibly swift. Within just 10 minutes, not only had they addressed my concern, but they also committed the fix to GitHub. I was thoroughly impressed by the speed and efficiency of the team's action. It's rare to receive such prompt and effective support. This experience has certainly raised my expectations for customer service in the tech industry. Kudos to the team for their exemplary service!</p></div></div></div><div class="min-w-0 flex-[0_0_min(100%,300px)]"><div class="h-full cursor-pointer border-l p-4 pt-5 shadow-sm transition-shadow line-clamp-2 text-sm text-foreground/60">Supastarter is truly an exceptional startup template! It boasts an impressive structure and exudes professionalism right from the start. The authentication options provided are top-notch, seamlessly integrated into the system. Additionally, the databases and other components are designed with efficiency and effectiveness in mind. The thoughtful consideration for serverless solutions is evident, showcasing a forward-thinking approach to modern development challenges. I adore the layout and highly recommend it as a startup template to others. It has saved me a tremendous amount of initial time in my project, proving to be an invaluable asset.</p></div></div></div><div class="min-w-0 flex-[0_0_min(100%,300px)]"><div class="h-full &amp; Freelance Software Engineer</small></div></div><div><p class="mt-2 mb-0 line-clamp-2 text-sm text-foreground/60">Supastarter helped a ton with getting my project off the ground! You get authentication, prisma db connections, migrations, marketing and saas folders for your app, the damn complex payment subscription hooks setup. 

It's gonna save you weeks of having to slouch through your side project because Stripe hook doesn't work / connecting your data to your users is hard. 

At this price, Supastarter is unbeatable in helping you cut down on development time and speed 🚀</p></div></div></div><div class="min-w-0 flex-[0_0_min(100%,300px)]"><div class="h-full cursor-pointer For example, a billing instruction can apply only to payment code, while UI instructions can require existing components and accessibility checks.\"}]\n2f:[\"$\",\"h2\",null,{\"children\":\"What does a good Copilot SaaS task look like?\"}]\n30:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"Give Copilot one product capability, point it at an existing pattern, define data ownership, and state the checks it must run. For example:\"}]\n31:[\"$\",\"blockquote\",null,{\"children\":[\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":[\"Read \",[\"$\",\"code\",null,{\"className\":\"not-prose rounded-[8px] border border-primary/20 bg-primary/5 px-1 py-0.5 text-sm font-normal text-foreground/80 [pre\u003e\u0026]:border-0 [pre\u003e\u0026]:bg-transparent [pre\u003e\u0026]:p-0\",\"children\":\"AGENTS.md\"}],\" and inspect the existing organization-scoped API procedures. Add project usage limits: store a monthly project allowance on each plan, prevent project creation after the active organization reaches its limit, return a typed error, and show an upgrade message in the project form. Add tests for allowed and blocked creation. Do not change checkout or webhook handling. Run lint and the relevant tests before finishing.\"]}],\"\\n\"]}]\n32:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"That prompt answers the questions agents otherwise
